## Abstract

 __Click to copy section link Section link copied!

[High Resolution
Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0014.jpeg
"High Resolution Image")

[Download MS PowerPoint
Slide](/action/downloadFigures?doi=10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&id=_i1
"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

Gold(I) derivatives of nitronyl nitroxide (NN) stabilized by complexation with
phosphine ligands were designed to promote dimerization of the paramagnets in
the solid state via Au–Au bonding. The complexes were successfully synthesized
by the reaction of the corresponding phosphine [_n_ -Bu3P or (4-FC6H4)3P],
AuCl(THT), and NN–H under basic conditions in high yields. The gold(I)
complexes were characterized by cyclic voltammetry, electron spin resonance,
infrared, and UV/vis spectroscopy supported by quantum chemical calculations.
Crystallographic analyses of the complexes showed that they possess Au–Au
bonds (2.930 and 3.095 Å). The Au–Au bonding was confirmed by density
functional theory calculations and quantum theory of “atoms in molecules” and
natural bond orbital analyses, which made it possible to determine bond
critical points (3, −1) and Au–Au bond orders (0.36 and 0.23) as well as to
estimate the orbital contribution to the energy of Au–Au bonds (12.0 and 8.6
kcal/mol).

This publication is licensed under the terms of your institutional
subscription. [Request reuse
permissions.](/servlet/linkout?type=rightslink&url=startPage%3D1710%26pageCount%3D11%26copyright%3DAmerican%2BChemical%2BSociety%26author%3DIgor%2BA.%2BZayakin%252C%2BAlexander%2BA.%2BKorlyukov%252C%2BDmitry%2BE.%2BGorbunov%252C%2Bet%2Bal%26orderBeanReset%3Dtrue%26imprint%3DAmerican%2BChemical%2BSociety%26volumeNum%3D41%26issueNum%3D13%26contentID%3Dacs.organomet.2c00200%26title%3DAu%25E2%2580%2593Au%2BChemical%2BBonding%2Bin%2BNitronyl%2BNitroxide%2BGold%2528I%2529%2BDerivatives%26numPages%3D11%26pa%3D%26issn%3D0276-7333%26publisherName%3Dacs%26publication%3Dorgnd7%26rpt%3Dn%26endPage%3D1720%26publicationDate%3DJuly%2B2022)

## Introduction

 __Click to copy section link Section link copied!

* * *

Metal–metal bonding (M–M) imparts important properties to functional
materials, for example, luminescence, polychromism, electrical conductivity,
or magnetism. [(1−3)](javascript:void\(0\);) The reasons for the M–M bonding
are related to the relativistic effects inherent in heavy elements. Most
reports of metal–metal bonding deal with the solid state, where X-ray
diffraction (XRD) structural parameters provide accurate information about
metal–metal distances to reveal short ones. Such short distances are often
observed in gold(I) complexes, generally referred to as Au(I)–Au(I) bonding.
[(4−6)](javascript:void\(0\);) This type of bond occurs in all areas of gold
chemistry, especially in catalysis [(7)](javascript:void\(0\);) and
photoluminescence. [(8,9)](javascript:void\(0\);) Complexes containing P-donor
ligands occupy a prominent place in gold(I) coordination chemistry, and many
structurally characterized examples feature the expected Au(I)–Au(I) bonds.
[(10,11)](javascript:void\(0\);) It has been found that even coordination
compounds with an extended ligand framework assemble in the solid state in
such a way that the Au(I)–Au(I) bonds can contribute to overall stability.

Recently, a new trend has emerged in the chemistry of gold complexes with
phosphine ligands. This trend is related to the synthesis and study of the
properties of complexes in which a gold atom is bonded to a paramagnetic
moiety. The first examples of such complexes are specific derivatives of a
paramagnetic organogold unit with a phosphine ligand (PR3) of the NN–Au–PR3
type (where NN is a deprotonated nitronyl nitroxide moiety, i.e.,
4,4,5,5-tetramethylimidazoline-3-oxide-1-oxyl).
[(12−14)](javascript:void\(0\);) It has been revealed that they have unique
redox properties and can serve as an attractive cross-coupling reagent for
introducing the NN group into a reaction with aryl iodides and activated aryl
bromides. [(12,15−25)](javascript:void\(0\);)

Nitronyl nitroxide–gold derivatives (NN–Au–PR3) are air-stable and possess
high kinetic stability both in solution and in the solid state. An analysis of
their crystal structures has revealed that in NN–Au–PR3, the nitronyl
nitroxide moiety is prone to the formation of a large number of short
intermolecular contacts as compared to purely organic nitronyl nitroxides.
[(14)](javascript:void\(0\);) This tendency is due to the anionic nature of
the NN group, which effectively interacts with positively charged atoms of
neighboring molecules, thereby giving rise to a three-dimensional structure.
Moreover, the stability of NN–Au–PR3 paves a unique way to investigation into
Au(I)–Au(I) bonding and related phenomena in open-shell molecules. To study
Au–Au bonding, Japanese chemists have developed various dinuclear gold
complexes with _o_ -, _m_ -, and _p_ -phenylene linkers (Scheme 1). Among the
prepared complexes, the ortho-substituted derivatives in which Au(I)–Au(I)
bonds are clearly observed in an analysis of the crystal structure show
characteristic spin–spin interactions, electrochemical properties, and
solvatochromic behavior. [(26)](javascript:void\(0\);)

## Scheme 1

Scheme 1. Chemical Structures of Gold Complexes with _o_ -, _m_ -, and _p_
-Phenylene Linkers

[High Resolution
Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0009.jpeg
"High Resolution Image")

[Download MS PowerPoint
Slide](/action/downloadFigures?doi=10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&id=sch1
"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

It can be assumed that the geometry of diphosphine ligands in ortho-
substituted derivatives (Scheme 1) is the driving force behind Au(I)–Au(I)
bonding. Nonetheless, a question arises, can these Au(I)–Au(I) bonds be
realized in principle without support of the ligand backbone? In this paper,
we give an affirmative answer and describe the first nitronyl
nitroxide–gold(I)-phosphine complexes (NN–Au–PR3) capable of forming
Au(I)–Au(I) bonds in the solid state.

## Experimental and Computational Details

 __Click to copy section link Section link copied!

* * *

###  Chemicals and General Methods

4,4,5,5-Tetramethyl-4,5-dihydro-1 _H_ -imidazole-3-oxide-1-oxyl
[(27)](javascript:void\(0\);) was synthesized as reported earlier. Other
chemicals were of the highest purity commercially available and were used as
received. All the solvents were of reagent quality. Reactions were performed
in an inert atmosphere; their progress was monitored by thin-layer
chromatography on silica gel 60 F254 aluminum sheets from Merck. The
chromatography was carried out using silica gel (0.050–0.160 mm) for column
chromatography.

###  High-Resolution Mass Spectrometry (HRMS)

HRMS was performed using a Bruker micrOTOF II instrument in positive ion
electrospray ionization (ESI) mode at 4500 V (_m_ /_z_ 50–1600) with internal
calibration by means of the ESI Tuning Mix (Agilent). The carrier gas (N2)
flow rate was 3 μL·min–1, and the interface temperature was 200 °C. Fourier
transform infrared (FTIR) spectra were registered using a BRUKER Vertex-70
FTIR spectrometer. UV–vis spectra were recorded on an Agilent 8453 for 10–4 M
solutions in acetonitrile in a 10 mm quartz cuvette with a Teflon cap.
Electron paramagnetic resonance (EPR) spectra were acquired in an oxygen-free
diluted toluene solution at 295 K at concentrations of ∼10–4 M by means of a
Bruker EMX 6/1 X Band spectrometer equipped with an ER4102ST standard
rectangular TE012 resonator having a center frequency of 9.8 GHz. A standard
DPPH sample was used for determining isotropic g-factors (_g_ iso). The
spectra were simulated in the Winsim v.0.96 software
[(28)](javascript:void\(0\);) according to a previously adopted procedure.
[(29)](javascript:void\(0\);)

###  Cyclic Voltammetry Measurements

Cyclic voltammetry measurements for gold derivatives **1i,j** were performed
in a dry glove box with an argon atmosphere at humidity and oxygen levels not
exceeding 1 ppm. The analyzed compounds dissolved at 0.002 M in a supporting
electrolyte (see below) were subjected to electrochemical transformations in a
standard three-electrode glass cell at a potential sweep rate of 0.05–1.00
V·s–1. The working electrode was a glassy carbon disc electrode with a disc
diameter of 1.7 mm. Before use, its surface was polished with sandpaper and
then by means of chromium(III) oxide paste to achieve a mirror finish. The
auxiliary electrode was a platinum wire calcined in a gas burner flame to
remove oxides and other possible contaminants from the surface. The potentials
of the studied processes were measured relative to a reference electrode,
which was a silver wire coated with a layer of silver chloride, separated from
the main volume of the electrolyte by an electrolytic bridge filled with a
solution of the supporting electrolyte. The reference electrode was calibrated
against a ferrocene/ferrocenium couple (_E_ 0 = 0.400 V with respect to a
normal hydrogen electrode). The supporting electrolyte was a 0.1 M solution of
Bu4NBF4 (99%, Sigma-Aldrich) in acetonitrile (≥99.9%, HPLC gradient grade,
Fisher Chemical) with a water content not exceeding 20 ppm, according to Karl
Fischer titration, and a Mettler-Toledo titrator C10SD was employed.

#####  (4,4,5,5-Tetramethyl-4,5-dihydro-1 _H_
-imidazole-3-oxide-1-oxyl-2-ido)(tris(4-fluorophenyl)phosphine)gold
(NN–Au–PFPh3) (**1j**)

Tris(4-fluorophenyl)phosphine (0.050 g, 0.156 mmol) was added to a CH2Cl2 (3
mL) solution of chloro(tetrahydrothiophene)gold(I) (0.050 g, 0.156 mmol) under
argon. After stirring for 6 h, NN–H (0.025 g, 0.156 mmol) and NaOH (0.007 g,
0.175 mmol) dissolved in MeOH (0.3 mL) were sequentially introduced into the
reaction mixture. The solvent was evaporated under reduced pressure after
stirring for 2 h. CH2Cl2 was added to the residue, and the mixture was passed
through a glass filter. To the filtrate, _n_ -heptane was added, and the
solution was evaporated until the product started precipitating. The mixture
was kept at −10 °C for 30 min. The organogold derivative was filtered and
rinsed with hexane. Yield: 0.1 g (95%), indigo-blue powder. Crystals suitable
for X-ray structural analysis were obtained by slow recrystallization from
acetone/_n_ -heptane. Electron spin resonance (ESR) spectroscopy: _g_ =
2.0071, A(2 N) = 0.772 mT, A(P) = 0.226 mT, recorded in toluene at room
temperature. Infrared (IR) spectroscopy (KBr, cm–1): 3434, 3095, 3053, 2980,
2928, 2855, 2556, 2400, 2355, 2199, 2063, 1903, 1733, 1685, 1648, 1589, 1535,
1496, 1396, 1367, 1323, 1233, 1161, 1138, 1100, 1012, 945, 829, 739, 710, 638,
616, 530, 490, 447. HRMS calcd for C25H25AuF3N2O2P [M + H]+: 670.1271. Found:
670.1244. Elemental analysis for C25H24AuF3N2O2P·H2O (%): calcd: C 43.7, H
3.8, N 4.1. Found: C 43.3, H 3.9, N 3.9.

#####  (4,4,5,5-Tetramethyl-4,5-dihydro-1 _H_
-imidazole-3-oxide-1-oxyl-2-ido)(tri-_n_ -buthylphosphine)gold (NN–Au–P _n_
Bu3) (**1i**)

(4,4,5,5-Tetramethyl-4,5-dihydro-1 _H_ -imidazole-3-oxide-1-oxyl-2-ido)(tri-
_n_ -buthylphosphine)gold (NN–Au–P _n_ Bu3) (**1i**) was obtained by a
technique analogous to the one described above. Yield: 0.170 g (98%), indigo-
blue powder. Crystals suitable for X-ray structural analysis were prepared by
slow recrystallization from CH2Cl2/_n_ -heptane. ESR: _g_ = 2.0072, _A_(2 N) =
0.767 mT, _A_(P) = 0.232 mT, recorded in toluene at room temperature. IR (KBr,

calcd: C 41.1, H 7.1, N 5.0. Found: C 40.7, H 7.3, N 4.6.

####  X-ray Crystallography Experiments

Single-crystal X-ray analyses of {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2 were carried out at
the Center for Molecule Composition Studies (A.N. Nesmeyanov Institute of
Organoelement Compounds of Russian Academy of Sciences). The structures were
solved by the direct method and refined in anisotropic approximation for
nonhydrogen atoms. Hydrogen atoms of methyl, methylene, and aromatic groups
were calculated according to those of idealized geometry and refined with
constraints applied to C–H and O–H bond lengths and equivalent displacement
parameters [Ueq(H) = 1.2Ueq(X), X: the central atom of the XH2 group; Ueq(H) =
1.5Ueq(Y), Y: the central atom of the YH3 group]. All structures were solved
with the ShelXT [(30)](javascript:void\(0\);) software and refined in ShelXL.
[(31)](javascript:void\(0\);) Molecular graphics were drawn with the help of
the OLEX2 [(32)](javascript:void\(0\);) software.

CCDC
[2165806](https://www.ccdc.cam.ac.uk/services/structure_request?pid=ccdc:2165806&issn=0276-7333&id=doi:10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&sid=ACS)
and
[2165807](https://www.ccdc.cam.ac.uk/services/structure_request?pid=ccdc:2165807&issn=0276-7333&id=doi:10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&sid=ACS)
contain supplementary crystallographic data for {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2. These
data can be obtained free of charge from The Cambridge Crystallographic Data
Centre via <https://www.ccdc.cam.ac.uk/structures>.

###  Computational Details

Geometry optimization for nitronyl nitroxide radicals **1i** and **1j** and
their dimers was performed at the dispersion-corrected B97-D3 level
[(33,34)](javascript:void\(0\);) with the def2-TZVP basis set (with ECP for
Au). [(35)](javascript:void\(0\);) The dispersion-corrected calculations were
based on the Becke–Johnson damping function. [(36)](javascript:void\(0\);)
Optimizations were carried out both in the gas phase and in solution; the
solvent was taken into account using the CPCM model.
[(37)](javascript:void\(0\);)

A number of theoretical methods were utilized to analyze the electronic
structure of radicals **1i** and **1j** and their dimers {**1i**}2 and
{**1j**}2. For XRD geometric parameters, quantum theory of atoms in molecules
(QTAIM) topological descriptors at bond critical points (BCPs)
[(38,39)](javascript:void\(0\);) were calculated using the Multiwfn software
(version 3.2) [(40)](javascript:void\(0\);) and the results of all-electron
calculations with the scalar relativistic Douglas–Kroll–Hess (DKH2)
Hamiltonian [(41)](javascript:void\(0\);) and the B3LYP functional.
[(42,43)](javascript:void\(0\);) The WTBS basis set for Au
[(44)](javascript:void\(0\);) and the DKH-def2-TZVP basis set
[(45)](javascript:void\(0\);) for all other atoms were employed in these
calculations.

In addition, the natural bond orbital (NBO) [(46,47)](javascript:void\(0\);)
analysis was performed in the NBO7 software [(48,49)](javascript:void\(0\);)
and _GAUSSIAN16_ software suite. [(50)](javascript:void\(0\);) In the NBO
procedure, radicals **1i** and **1j** were divided into three fragments: Au+
ions with the d [(10)](javascript:void\(0\);) valence shell, an NN anion, and
the closed-shell PR3 ligand; each respective dimer was divided into six
fragments. To estimate the orbital contribution to Au–P, Au–C, and Au–Au bond
energies, the second-order perturbation energy was calculated:

where ΦD and ΦA as well as and εD and εA are the Lewis-type NBOs of donor (D)
and non-Lewis type NBOs of an acceptor (A) and their energies, respectively;
_n_ D is the occupation number of the donor NBO, and _F̂_ is a Fock operator.

Parameters _J_ of the exchange interaction () between radical fragments
(**1i** and **1j**) of dimers and between radical fragments of neighboring
dimers were calculated by the spin-unrestricted broken-symmetry approach
[(51)](javascript:void\(0\);) at the UB3LYP/def2-TZVP (with ECP for Au) level
using the formula [(52)](javascript:void\(0\);)

where indices HS and LS correspond to high-spin and broken-symmetry low-spin
solutions, respectively. Singlet–triplet splitting in the diradicals (_E_ ST =
2 J) was also calculated by the CASSCF and CASSCF/NEVPT2 procedures
[(53,54)](javascript:void\(0\);) with the same basis set.

Electronic absorption spectra of radicals **1i,j** were calculated via the
time-dependent density functional theory (DFT) approach
[(55)](javascript:void\(0\);) with the B3LYP functional and the def2-TZVP
basis set (with ECP for Au). Most of the aforementioned calculations were
performed using the ORCA 4.2.1 software suite. [(56)](javascript:void\(0\);)

The hyperfine coupling (HFC) tensor and constant (_A_ iso) with a gold nucleus
as well as with nitrogen and phosphorus nuclei were calculated by the exact
two-component relativistic X2C method [(57,58)](javascript:void\(0\);) with a
self-consistent account of spin–orbit coupling (SOC) implemented in the
ADF2019 software package. [(59−61)](javascript:void\(0\);) These computations
were performed with the B3LYP functional, the STO-type TZ2P-J basis set,
[(62)](javascript:void\(0\);) and the nonpoint model for nuclei.
[(63)](javascript:void\(0\);) A collinear approximation of spin polarization
was used in the case of the SOC. [(64)](javascript:void\(0\);) The spherical
Gaussian nuclear charge distribution model [(65)](javascript:void\(0\);) was
utilized for accurate predictions of the HFC constants.

## Results and Discussion

 __Click to copy section link Section link copied!

* * *

To date, a fairly wide range of mononuclear Au(I) derivatives of a nitronyl
nitroxide radical **1a–h** , **2** , and **3** has been obtained (Scheme 2).
An inspection of their crystallographic data did not reveal the presence of
Au(I)–Au(I) bonds (Table 1). Indeed, in all cases, the distance between the
gold centers is noticeably larger than the sum of their van der Waals (vdW)
radii proposed by Alvarez (4.64 Å). [(66)](javascript:void\(0\);)

On the other hand, in the crystalline state, the vast majority of linear
gold(I) complexes were found to be in the form of oligomers or polymers. The
most common oligomers turned out to be dimers, trimers, and tetramers, whereas
one-dimensional chains are the most common polymers. The complexes remain in
the monomeric form only when steric bulkiness of the substituents at the gold
center prevents the Au–Au bonding. [(4)](javascript:void\(0\);) Therefore, to
increase the propensity of NN–Au–PR3 complexes for the Au–Au bonding, we
decided to synthesize the NN–Au–P _n_ Bu3 (**1i**) complex, in which instead
of bulky _tert_ -butyl groups, the phosphorus atom is surrounded by _n_ -butyl
substituents. The choice of the structure of the second compound is not so
obvious and is rather the fruit of intuition. For instance, it has been
noticed that in a series of Ar–Au–PPh3 complexes (**4**), the introduction of
substituent(s) R having a positive M effect, e.g., a methoxy group or a
fluorine atom, leads to the formation of dimers {Ar–Au–PPh3}2 through the
Au(I)···Au(I) bonding in solid phases. [(67)](javascript:void\(0\);) We
thought that it is expedient to proceed in a similar way, namely, to
synthesize the derivative NN–Au–PFPh3 (**1j**) containing para-fluorinated
phenyl rings and to study its crystal structure.

Gold derivatives of the nitronyl nitroxide, NN–Au–P _n_ Bu3 (**1i**) and
NN–Au–PFPh3 (**1j**), were obtained in a high yield (95–98%) upon treatment
with a base solution (NaOH/MeOH) containing equivalent amounts of NN–H and
Cl–Au–PR3 generated in situ from chloro(tetrahydrothiophene)gold and the
corresponding phosphine (Scheme 3). Analytically pure samples of complexes
**1i,j** were prepared by chromatography on silica gel and recrystallization
from solvents suitable for the growth of crystals.

## Scheme 3

Scheme 3. Preparation of Gold Derivatives of the Nitronyl Nitroxide: **1i,j**

Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0011.jpeg

"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

To understand in detail the electronic structure of the gold derivatives of
nitronyl nitroxide radicals, a number of experimental and theoretical methods
were employed. Figure 1 shows experimental EPR spectra of radicals **1i** and
**1j** , as recorded in dilute degassed toluene solutions at room temperature,
and their simulation. Isotropic g values were determined using solid DPPH as a
reference, and accuracy levels of determining HFC interaction constants and g
values were estimated as 0.005 mT and 0.0001, respectively. The spectra of
**1i** and **1j** are very similar and are characterized by predominant
couplings with two equivalent nitrogen atoms and one phosphorus (_I_ = 1/2),
producing a quintet of doublets with slightly higher g values than typical for
purely organic R–NNs. [(25)](javascript:void\(0\);) As in earlier studies,
[(14)](javascript:void\(0\);) the spectra showed no signs of couplings with
gold, although the only stable natural isotope 197Au is magnetic with _I_ =
3/2.

## Figure 1

Figure 1. Model (top) and experimental (bottom) EPR spectra for degassed
toluene solutions (10–4 M) of radicals (a) **1i** and (b) **1j** at 295 K.
Parameters of the model EPR spectra: (a) _A_ iso(2 N) = 0.772 mT, _A_ iso(P) =
0.226 mT, and _g_ iso = 2.0071; (b) _A_ iso(2 N) = 0.767 mT, _A_ iso(P) =
0.232 mT, and _g_ iso = 2.0072.

[High Resolution
Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0002.jpeg
"High Resolution Image")

[Download MS PowerPoint
Slide](/action/downloadFigures?doi=10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&id=fig1
"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

To understand the absence of a hyperfine interaction with 197Au, or at least
of its manifestation in the EPR spectrum, we calculated spin-Hamiltonian
parameters by the exact two-component relativistic X2C method with a self-
consistent account of the SOC at the DFT level. Because the EPR spectra of
radicals **1i** and **1j** are very similar (Figure 1), we performed the
computations only for the optimized structure of **1i**. These calculations
predicted the following parameters for **1i** : _g_ iso = 2.0099, _A_ iso(2 N)
= 0.584 mT, _A_ iso(P) = 0.410 mT, and _A_ iso(197Au) = 0.535 mT. A comparison
with the experiment showed that _g_ iso is noticeably overestimated, whereas
_A_ iso(N) is reproduced fairly well, although─as usual at the DFT level
[(25)](javascript:void\(0\);)─it is slightly underestimated, and _A_ iso(P) is
almost twice the experimental value. Most challenging, _A_ iso(197Au) was
predicted to be quite high.

It should be noted that our computational approach recently manifested perfect
agreement with the experiment for the HFC tensor with 125Te, 187Re, and 127I
nuclei. [(22,68,69)](javascript:void\(0\);) It is also important to mention
that hyperfine interactions with the above-mentioned relativistic nuclei have
been recorded either only at cryogenic temperatures
[(68,69)](javascript:void\(0\);) or at elevated temperatures.
[(22)](javascript:void\(0\);) The last result was explained by assuming
substantially different characteristic times for averaging the anisotropy of
the hyperfine-interaction tensors with nonrelativistic and relativistic
nuclei: for the former, the anisotropy is due to the dipole–dipole
interaction, whereas for the latter, a significant contribution to the
anisotropy is due to SOC. [(22)](javascript:void\(0\);) It cannot be ruled out
that in our case too, the hyperfine interaction with 197Au does not manifest
itself as a splitting in the EPR spectrum owing to the slow averaging of
anisotropic interactions arising from SOC.

Figure 2 displays UV–vis spectra of radicals **1i,j**. Both spectra have
similar long-wavelength bands with pronounced fine structure and maxima at
∼570 nm. Both spectra also contain a highly intense band with maxima at 363
and 372 nm for **1i** and **1j** , respectively. It is clear that at shorter
wavelengths (<250 nm), radical **1j** with the P(Ar)3 substituent absorbs much
more strongly due to the participation in electronic transitions of the aryl
group. It is well known [(25)](javascript:void\(0\);) that UV–vis spectra of
2-substituted nitronyl nitroxides with alkyl, aryl, or heterocyclic
substituents (NN–R) yield a long-wavelength absorption band with clear-cut
fine structure and a peak at 560–650 nm. To explain this similarity, we
performed the time-dependent DFT calculation of the UV–vis spectrum of radical
**1i**.

## Figure 2

Figure 2. Experimental UV–vis spectra of acetonitrile solutions (10–4 M) of
radicals **1i** (red spectrum) and **1j** (blue spectrum) recorded at room
temperature (the inset shows the enlarged spectra) and positions and
oscillator strengths (red bars) of electronic transitions for **1i** , as
calculated at the TD-UB3LYP/def2-TZVP level. The information above the arrows
indicates the contribution of one-electron excitations to the wavefunction of
the corresponding excited state.

[High Resolution
Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0003.jpeg
"High Resolution Image")

[Download MS PowerPoint
Slide](/action/downloadFigures?doi=10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&id=fig2
"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

Figure 2 shows that the calculations well reproduce the UV–vis spectrum of
**1i**. As in the case of NN–R (Alk, Ar, or heterocycles),
[(25,70)](javascript:void\(0\);) only one electronic transition is responsible
for the long-wavelength band with the maximum at 570 nm. This transition
involves a sum of the promotions of an α-electron from the singly occupied
molecular orbital (SOMO) to the lowest unoccupied orbital (a) and of a
β-electron from the HOMO to the β counterpart of the SOMO (b) (Figure 3).
Thus, the fine structure of this band results from vibrational progression.
Similarly, to a pyrazolyl-substituted NN, for which the C–C stretching mode
appears in the spectrum, [(70)](javascript:void\(0\);) progression of the C–Au
stretching vibration is most likely observed for **1i**. The main contribution
to the second band with a maximum at 363 nm is made by a similar electronic
transition: a negative combination of (a) and (b). Note that two _n_ –π*
transitions were predicted at 430 and 377 nm but with very low oscillator
strength (0.9 to 2.9) × 10–4; accordingly, there is no sign of them in the
spectrum (Figure 2). Thus, the electronic structure of the gold-substituted NN
is very similar to that of NN–R (R = Alk, Ar, or a heterocycle).

## Figure 3

Figure 3. Molecular orbitals of **1i** as calculated at the UB3LYP/def2-TZVP
level and the excitations that make the main contribution to the absorption
bands marked in Figure 2.

"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

For open-shell systems, such as radicals **1i,j** , the energies of SOMOs and
LUMOs predetermine the redox properties of the compounds. We, therefore,
investigated oxidation potentials for the synthesized nitroxides by cyclic
voltammetry using a three-electrode system with Fc/Fc+ as the reference.
Figure 4 shows a cyclic voltammogram of **1i** covering the potential range
available in acetonitrile as a solvent. One can see that the first one-
electron oxidation peak at 0.11 V, assigned to the NN moiety, is accompanied
by two response peaks. This means that the product of electrochemical
oxidation of **1i** (the corresponding cation **1i +**) enters into a
subsequent reversible chemical reaction with the formation of another
electrochemically active compound **1i +′**, which, like **1i +**, is reduced
during reverse potential scanning. At various potential sweep rates (0.05 to
1.0 V/s), the interval between the direct and first response peaks proved to
be 0.059 V, and the sum of currents in the response of reduction peaks to the
current in the direct oxidation peak does not depend on the potential sweep
rate and was found to be 0.96, which indicates electrochemical reversibility
of the process ([Figure
S3](/doi/suppl/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/suppl_file/om2c00200_si_001.pdf)).
Aside from the said process, the cyclic-voltammetry curve also reflects
irreversible electrochemical processes occurring at potentials of 2.29 and
−1.68 V (Figure 4).

## Figure 4

Figure 4. Cyclic voltammograms of **1i** (2.2 × 10–3 M) at 0.1 V/s registered
in 0.1 M Bu4NBF4/MeCN vs Fc/Fc+ (the colored curve denotes the first oxidation
process).

[High Resolution
Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0005.jpeg
"High Resolution Image")

[Download MS PowerPoint
Slide](/action/downloadFigures?doi=10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&id=fig4
"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

The one-electron oxidation of nitroxide **1j** proceeds at a peak potential of
0.14 V, i.e., slightly higher potential than in the case of radical **1i**
([Figure
S1](/doi/suppl/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/suppl_file/om2c00200_si_001.pdf)).
The reverse process, regardless of the potential sweep rate (0.05 to 1.00
V/s), corresponds to a peak that is 0.062 V away from the direct one, which is
close to a theoretical value of 0.059 V for an electrochemically reversible
process. Furthermore, the ratio of the currents of the reverse and forward
peaks, which is close to 0.91, is suggestive of electrochemical and chemical
reversibility of the oxidation of nitroxide **1j** ([Figure
S2](/doi/suppl/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/suppl_file/om2c00200_si_001.pdf)).
For this process, forward and reverse peak potentials (without the effect of
uncompensated resistance) and half-wave potential are _E_ Ox = 0.134, _E_ Red
= 0.072 V, and _E_ 1/2 = 0.103 V, respectively. As for the peaks corresponding
to irreversible processes, there are several: on the oxidation curve at a
potential of 2.29 V and on the reduction curve at −1.64 and −2.95 V. On the
whole, excellent agreement was observed between the oxidation potentials of
**1i,j** (Table 2) and those of previously studied nitronyl nitroxide–gold
derivatives. [(13,14,26)](javascript:void\(0\);)

Single crystals of complexes NN–Au–P _n_ Bu3 (**1i**) and NN–Au–PFPh3 (**1j**)
(as crystallohydrate **1j** ·2H2O) were obtained by recrystallization from a
mixture of CH2Cl2 with _n_ -heptane or a mixture of acetone with _n_ -heptane,
respectively. Inspection of the crystallographic data for the presence of the
Au(I)···Au(I) bonding in the crystal structures of **1i** and **1j** uncovered
the formation of dimerized molecules. Structures of dimerized radicals
{**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2 are shown in Figure 5.

## Figure 5

In dimers {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2, the coordination polyhedron of Au atoms
could be described as T-shaped. Indeed, C2–Au–P angles were found to be close
to 180°, and P–Au–Au′–P′ dihedral angles close to 90° (Table 3). C2–Au bond
lengths for complexes **1i,j** turned out to be 2.035–2.040 Å, which are
similar to those of previously described NN–Au–PPh3 radicals (**1a**) (2.03
Å). [(12)](javascript:void\(0\);) N–O bond lengths for the NN moieties proved
to be 1.28–1.30 Å, which are typical of the corresponding bond lengths of NN
radicals. [(71)](javascript:void\(0\);)

Figure 6 shows a fragment of the crystal structure of the NN–Au–PFPh3·2H2O
hydrate complex (**1j** ·2H2O). In **1j** dimers, the angle between the planes
of the paramagnetic moieties is 7°, and the shortest O···O distance is 3.376
Å, which is considerably greater than the sum of vdW radii of O atoms (3.04 Å
[(72)](javascript:void\(0\);)). Dimers {**1j**}2 and disordered water
molecules (Figure 6) are assembled into a chain via ONO···H–OH hydrogen bonds
(d(O···H) = 1.89 Å, d(O...O) 2.713(3) Å, ∠O···H–O = 167°, on average) and
short contacts OH2O...H′CH3 (2.16 Å) and HH2O...O′NO (2.37 Å). Such an
assembly makes the shortest interdimer distances between the nitroxide O atoms
(4.964 Å) only slightly greater than distances between the parallel planes of
nitronyl nitroxide moieties (4.953 Å). In other words, these moieties lie
opposite each other, formally giving rise to a columnar structure extended
along the _c_ direction. The formation of such a crystal structure was driven
by the inclusion of water molecules, which was ensured by the growth of
crystals in the acetone– _n_ -heptane system. By contrast, when the mixture of
CH2Cl2 and _n_ -heptane was used, complex **1j** precipitated as oily
droplets.

## Figure 6

well as H-bonds ONO···H (purple dotted lines) and short contacts OH2O···H′CH3
and HH2O···O′NO (green dotted lines).

[High Resolution
Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0007.jpeg
"High Resolution Image")

[Download MS PowerPoint
Slide](/action/downloadFigures?doi=10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&id=fig6
"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

Well-shaped crystals of compound NN–Au–P _n_ Bu3 (**1i**) were obtained in the
mixture of CH2Cl2 and _n_ -heptane. According to XRD data, in dimers
{**1i**}2, the angle between the planes of the nitronyl nitroxide moieties is
10°, and the shortest O···O distance is 3.777 Å, which is considerably larger
than the sum of vdW radii of O atoms (3.04 Å). Given that there are no water
molecules in the structure of complex **1i** , its packing differs
significantly from that of compound **1j** (Figure 7).

## Figure 7

Figure 7. Fragment of the crystal structure of **1i** , and short
intermolecular distances HBu···ONO (2.428–2.718 Å, green dotted lines).

[High Resolution
Image](/cms/10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200/asset/images/large/om2c00200_0008.jpeg
"High Resolution Image")

[Download MS PowerPoint
Slide](/action/downloadFigures?doi=10.1021/acs.organomet.2c00200&id=fig7
"Download MS PowerPoint Slide")

Nevertheless, the most interesting for us is spatial and electronic structure
of dimers {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2. Distances between the gold centers in these
dimers (3.095 and 2.930 Å for {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2, respectively) are less
than the sum of their vdW radii but greater than the sum of their covalent
radii (2.72 Å). It should be pointed out that according to different
approaches, several values of the vdW radius for gold have been proposed so
far, and they range from previously established 1.66 Å
[(73)](javascript:void\(0\);) to recently accepted 2.32 Å.
[(66)](javascript:void\(0\);) To confirm the presence of weak noncovalent
bonding interactions in the dimers {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2 and to estimate
their energy, we carried out all-electron DFT calculations with the scalar
relativistic DKH2 Hamiltonian as described in the Computational Details
Section, using which we performed the QTAIM and NBO analyses. Because the C–Au
and P–Au coordination bonds in paramagnetic species are still studied
insufficiently, we also characterized these bonds. Table 3 presents selected
results of our DFT calculations for the dimers as well as the results of a
topological analysis of the electron density distribution and estimates of
bonding energies according to the NBO analysis and second-order perturbation
theory.

As expected, the (3, −1) BCPs were localized for Au–P and Au–C coordination
bonds, but more importantly, the QTAIM topological analysis did reveal the
presence of the (3, −1) BCPs for the Au(I)–Au(I) bonding in the **1i** and
**1j** crystal structures (Table 4). The coordination bonds Au–P and Au–C are
characterized by moderate electron densities (0.11–0.13), rather large and
positive values of their Laplacian, and negative electronic energy densities
at BCPs. These values of the above-mentioned topological descriptors are
typical of donor–acceptor bonds between the seemingly closed-shell Au(I)
center and NN or phosphine ligands. Note that |_V_ b|/_G_ b values are in the
range between 1 and 2, implying their partial covalence.
[(74,75)](javascript:void\(0\);)

Table 4. Selected Experimental Bond Lengths (_d_ , Å), Mayer Bond Orders
(_P_), and QTAIM Topological Descriptors: Electron Density (ρb), Its Laplacian
(Δρb), Electronic Energy Densities (_H_ b), and the Ratio of Potential- and
Kinetic-Energy Densities (|_V_ b|/_G_ b) at the BCPs and Orbital Contributions
(_E_ orb) to the Bond Energies, Estimated Using the NBO Analysis and Second-
Order Perturbation Theory, and the Main Contributions (_E_ main(2)) to These
Valuesa

 | bond|  _d_ , Å|  _P_|  ρb| ∇2ρb|  _H_ b| |_V_ b|/_G_ b|  _E_ orb = ∑ _E_(2), kcal/mol|  _E_ main(2), kcal/mol  
---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---  
**1i**|  Au–Au| 3.095| 0.23| 0.020| 0.066| 0.000| 1.000| 8.3|  
Au–C| 2.035| 0.65| 0.130| 0.368| –0.053| 1.367| 191.5| 181.5  
Au–P| 2.285| 0.80| 0.111| 0.179| –0.048| 1.517| 150.5| 144.6  
**1j**|  Au–Au| 2.930| 0.36| 0.028| 0.097| –0.001| 1.043| 12.1|  
Au–C| 2.038| 0.67| 0.129| 0.359| –0.053| 1.372| 185.6| 173.5  
Au–P| 2.286| 0.80| 0.109| 0.182| –0.047| 1.507| 137.8| 131.3  
  
a

ρb and Δρb are given in a.u., that is, _e_ /_a_ 0 [(3)](javascript:void\(0\);)
and _e_ /_a_ 0, [(5)](javascript:void\(0\);) respectively.

The Au–Au bonding in crystal structures of **1i** and **1j** features low
electron densities, low positive values of their Laplacian, and close-to-zero
or zero energy density at the BCPs (Table 4), which is typical of metal–metal
bonding in similar chemical systems. [(76−78)](javascript:void\(0\);) The low
values of the aforementioned topological descriptors for Au–Au bonds are in
line with the low Mayer bond orders calculated for them (Table 4).

With the help of the NBO analysis and second-order perturbation theory, we
estimated the orbital contribution to the energies of the bonds in question.
Note that these values are used only to qualitatively assess the relative
strength of different bond types. Table 4 shows that the orbital contribution
to the Au–C bond energy is huge, whereas the orbital contribution to the Au–P
bond energy is noticeably smaller. In both cases, the predominant contribution
is made by one pair of orbitals. Specifically, the interaction of the lone
pair of the P atom (donor) with the 6s orbital on Au(I) (acceptor) makes ∼95%
of the total orbital contribution to the Au–P bond energy. A similar
proportion (∼95%) is given by the interaction of the lone pair on the C atom
(NN group) with the Au(I) 6s orbital.

In the case of the Au–Au bonding, the orbital contribution is very small,
which, along with other parameters listed in Table 4, points to low energy of
this bond. In this context, the main contribution comes from the interaction
of the 5d orbitals of one Au(I) ion with the 6s orbital of another Au(I).

The structures of {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2 were optimized at the B97-D3 level
both in the gas phase and in dichloromethane and acetonitrile solutions. In
all cases, the structures of both dimers correspond to minima on potential-
energy surfaces. For dimer {**1i**}2, predicted Gibbs free energy of formation
(Δ _G_ f0) is −5.1 and −4.6 kcal/mol in CH2Cl2 and CH3CN, respectively. These
values of Δ _G_ f0 correspond to _K_ eq equal to ∼5 × 103 and ∼2 × 103 M–1 at
room temperature in CH2Cl2 and CH3CN, respectively, and at a concentration of
∼10–4 M, the monomeric form in solution is favored. As for {**1j**}2, more
exothermic dimerization was predicted: computed Δ _G_ f0 was −8.9 and −7.7
kcal/mol, respectively. It must be mentioned that error in the calculation of
Δ _G_ f0 can be several kcal/mol. Therefore, the calculations do not
contradict the crystallization of radicals **1i** and **1j** as dimers and
their presence in dilute solutions in the monomeric form.

As described above, in the **1j** ·2H2O crystal structure, the dimers are
assembled into chains, whereas in the **1i** crystal structure, such chains
are absent. To predict the magnetic properties of both paramagnets, intra- and
interdimer exchange interactions were calculated at the BS-DFT level. For
dimer {**1i**}2, the calculations were also carried out at a higher level of
theory. Table 5 shows that for this dimer, BS-DFT predicts a moderate
antiferromagnetic exchange interaction (_J_ = −23.4 cm–1). This parameter may
be somewhat underestimated here because the calculation at a higher level
predicted a doubled value. For dimer {**1j**}2, BS-DFT predicted a much
stronger antiferromagnetic interaction (_J_ = −95.8 cm–1), which may be
attributed to both a more parallel arrangement of NN moieties and a shorter
Au–Au bond. These factors influence the mechanisms of exchange interactions
through space and through bonds, respectively.

Table 5. Parameters (_J_) of the Intramolecular Exchange Interaction for
Dimers {**1i**}2 and {**1j**}2 as Calculated at Different Levels of Theory

dimers|  _d_ min(O···O), Å|  _J_ , cm–1  
---|---|---  
BS-UB3LYP| CASSCF(2,2)/NEVPT2| CASSCF(2,4)/NEVPT2  
{**1i**}2| 3.777| –23.4| –20.2| –41.0  
{**1j**}2| 3.776| –95.8|  |    
  
Parameters of intermolecular exchange interactions were estimated only at the
BS-DFT level. In the **1j** ·2H2O crystal, _J_ for the interaction between
neighboring radical moieties within the chain was found to be low and
ferromagnetic (3.1 cm–1). In the **1i** crystal, all exchange interactions
between radical parts of neighboring dimers proved to be negligible.

## Conclusions

 __Click to copy section link Section link copied!

* * *

The coordination chemistry of gold(I) has been a rapidly developing field in
recent times, and the unique properties and applications of gold complexes,
especially in catalysis, medicine, and materials chemistry, made a huge
contribution to these scientific advances. A new research avenue has recently
emerged that deals with the preparation of gold(I) derivatives carrying
paramagnetic substituents as well as their application to the chemistry of
stable nitroxides. In this regard, we designed and synthesized Au(I)
mononuclear complexes (NN–Au–PR3) with a nitronyl nitroxide anionic ligand and
various neutral phosphine ligands [_n_ -Bu3P or (4-FC6H4)3P] and studied their
spatial and electronic structure and physicochemical properties. X-ray
structural analysis of the new complexes revealed that during crystallization,
the complexes tend to dimerize with the formation of Au(I)–Au(I) bonds (2.930
and 3.095 Å). According to quantum chemical calculations, the Au–Au bonds have
a low energy, to which the main contribution is made by the interaction of the
5d orbitals of one Au(I) ion with the 6s orbital of another Au(I). In the
solid state, the Au–Au bonding leads to close proximity of two radical
moieties that, in turn, gives rise to antiferromagnetic exchange interactions
between paramagnetic centers. In general, this opens new possibilities in
design of different exchange-coupled systems.

1

Supramolecular assembly of molecular gold(I) compounds: An evaluation of the
competition and complementarity between aurophilic (Au···Au) and conventional
hydrogen bonding interactions

Tiekink, Edward R. T.

Coordination Chemistry Reviews (2014), 275 (), 130-153CODEN: CCHRAM;
ISSN:0010-8545. (Elsevier B.V.)

A review. A bibliog. review of the crystallog. literature has been conducted
on gold(I) compds. bearing functional groups capable of forming conventional
hydrogen bonding interactions, namely, primary amide, secondary amide and
carboxylic acid. The clear majority of structures featured hydrogen bonding
interactions leading to well defined zero- or one-dimensional supramol.
architectures; rarely were two-dimensional architectures formed. Conversely,
aurophilic, Au···Au, were the exception rather than the rule. This observation
is correlated with steric hindrance: small atoms assocd. via directional
hydrogen bonding compared with encumbered gold-atom environments (due to bulky
ligands). Amide-{···HNCO}2 and carboxylic acid-{···HOCO}2 synthons featured in
significantly more of the gold(I) structures compared with all-org. systems,
particularly for non-ionic structures where charged-assisted hydrogen bonding
precluded the formation of these cyclic synthons. This suggests a crystal
engineering strategy of functionalizing metal complexes with hydrogen bonding
potential to improve opportunities of rational design of crystal structures.

2

Mercurophilic Interactions

Schmidbaur, Hubert; Schier, Annette

Organometallics (2015), 34 (11), 2048-2066CODEN: ORGND7; ISSN:0276-7333.
(American Chemical Society)

A review. The widely confirmed abundance of aurophilic interactions in the
structural chem. of gold(I) compds. gives reason to scrutinize the evidence
for analogous mercurophilic interactions in mercury(II) compds. more
sporadically advanced in the literature. From the inventory of early
observations and more recently accumulated data it appears that the equil.
distances of intra- and intermol. Hg-Hg contacts (ca. 3.5 Å) are generally
much larger than those of Au-Au contacts (ca. 3.0 Å). There are very few
estns. of the small energy contributions assocd. with the Hg-Hg contacts from
exptl. data, but quantum-chem. calcns. have confirmed that these energies also
are generally much lower than those for the Au-Au contacts. Notwithstanding,
there are several special cases where particularly short Hg-Hg distances, or
the preferred modes of assocns. of mols. into oligo- or polymeric arrays, do
indicate significant attractive mercurophilic interactions. Photophys.
phenomena also suggest that for several groups of oligomeric organomercury(II)
compds. there is a much stronger Hg-Hg interaction in the excited state, with
interesting consequences for the emissive properties. Interestingly, the most
intriguing observations that suggest mercurophilic interactions, like the
polymercuration of oxygen, nitrogen, and carbon, date back to the 19th century
and still call for a rationalization.

Argentophilic Interactions

Schmidbaur, Hubert; Schier, Annette

Angewandte Chemie, International Edition (2015), 54 (3), 746-784CODEN: ACIEF5;
ISSN:1433-7851. (Wiley-VCH Verlag GmbH & Co. KGaA)

A review. The decade 1990-2000 saw a growing interest in aurophilic
interactions in gold chem. These interactions were found to influence
significantly a variety of structural and other phys. characteristics of
gold(I) compds. The attention paid to this unusual and counterintuitive type
of intra- and intermol. bonding between seemingly closed-shell metal centers
has rapidly been extended to also include silver chem. Hundreds of exptl. and
computational studies have since been dedicated to the argentophilicity
phenomenon. The results of this development are reviewed herein focusing on
mol. systems where two or more silver(I) centers are in close contact leading
to specific structural characteristics and a variety of novel phys.
properties. These include strongly modified ligand-to-metal charge-transfer
processes obsd. in absorption and emission spectroscopy, but also colossal
pos. and neg. thermal expansion on the one hand and unprecedented neg. linear
compressibility of crystal parameters on the other.

A briefing on aurophilicity

Schmidbaur, Hubert; Schier, Annette

Chemical Society Reviews (2008), 37 (9), 1931-1951CODEN: CSRVBR;
ISSN:0306-0012. (Royal Society of Chemistry)

A review. There is now compelling exptl. evidence for the existence of
specific intra- and intermol. bonding between seemingly closed-shell gold(i)
centers (5d10) which manifests itself in all areas of gold chem. This
"aurophilic interaction", which had not been predicted by conventional valence
theory, was found to be assocd. with binding energies in some cases exceeding
even those of strong hydrogen bonds and therefore to be highly significant in
co-detg. mol. structure and dynamics. In high-level theor. treatments the
attraction is rationalized as a "super van der Waals bonding" based on
particularly strong relativistic, dispersion and correlation effects (crit.
review, 265 refs.).

5

Aurophilic interactions as a subject of current research: an up-date

Schmidbaur, Hubert; Schier, Annette

Chemical Society Reviews (2012), 41 (1), 370-412CODEN: CSRVBR; ISSN:0306-0012.
(Royal Society of Chemistry)

A review. Recently accumulated exptl. evidence for aurophilic interactions in
and between mol. Au(I) compds. and the results of pertinent theor. calcns. are
reviewed for the period from 2007 to mid-2011. The influence of the intra- and
intermol. bonding contacts between the closed-shell metal centers, Au-Au, on
the mol. and crystal structures, and the consequences of these effects for the
chem. and phys. properties of Au compds. are summarized for the various
classes of mono- and polynuclear systems. The literature survey builds on the
contents of previous reviews and relates new exptl. and theor. findings to
earlier observations (353 refs.).

6

The aurophilicity phenomenon: a decade of experimental findings, theoretical
concepts and emerging applications

Schmidbaur, Hubert

Gold Bulletin (London) (2000), 33 (1), 3-10CODEN: GOBUFW; ISSN:1027-8591.
(World Gold Council)

A review, with 111 refs., discusses aurophilicity. The term aurophilicity was
introduced in 1989 to describe phenomena in the structural chem. of Au which
could not be readily rationalized by conventional concepts of chem. bonding.
In the following decade the aurophilicity concept was widely applied and
supported by the results of many exptl. as well as theor. studies. It will be
carried over into the new millennium as a continued incentive for studies that
will help in the understanding of the unique properties of Au.

7

Dinuclear gold catalysis

Wang, Wenliang; Ji, Cheng-Long; Liu, Kai; Zhao, Chuan-Gang; Li, Weipeng; Xie,
Jin

Chemical Society Reviews (2021), 50 (3), 1874-1912CODEN: CSRVBR;
ISSN:0306-0012. (Royal Society of Chemistry)

A review. Gold chem. has developed extensively in the past decade, and a dozen
good reviews have been presented discussing this progress. Few however have
paid close attention to the progress in org. synthesis of dinuclear gold-
catalysis. A dinuclear gold catalyst is defined here as a gold complex with
two gold centers linked by a bidentate ligand. With theor. anal. and some
representative studies in recent years, this comprehensive review highlights
the particular properties of dinuclear gold-complexes, esp. aurophilic
interactions, and systematically summarizes the recent achievements of
dinuclear gold-catalyzed coupling reactions, asym. catalysis, and
photocatalysis, where dinuclear gold catalysts tend to show a greater
advantage than the mononuclear gold catalysts.

8

Luminescent gold(I) complexes for chemosensing

He, Xiaoming; Yam, Vivian Wing-Wah

Coordination Chemistry Reviews (2011), 255 (17-18), 2111-2123CODEN: CCHRAM;
ISSN:0010-8545. (Elsevier B.V.)

A review. With the rich spectroscopic and luminescence properties assocd. with
aurophilic Au···Au interactions, gold(I) complexes have provided an excellent
platform for the design of luminescent chemosensors. This review concs. on our
recent exploration of luminescent gold(I) complexes in host-guest chem.
Through the judicious design and choice of the functional receptor groups,
specific chemosensors for cations and/or anions have been obtained.
Utilization of sensing mechanisms based on the on-off switching of Au···Au
interactions and photoinduced electron transfer (PET) has been successfully
demonstrated. The two-coordinate nature of gold(I) complexes has also been
utilized for the design of ditopic receptors through connecting both cation-
and anion-binding sites within a single mol.

9

Luminescence behaviour of Au(I)-Cu(I) heterobimetallic coordination polymers
based on alkynyl-tris(2-pyridyl)phosphine Au(I) complexes

Petrovskii, Stanislav K.; Paderina, Aleksandra V.; Sizova, Anastasia A.;
Baranov, Andrey Yu.; Artem'ev, Alexander A.; Sizov, Vladimir V.; Grachova,
Elena V.

Dalton Transactions (2020), 49 (38), 13430-13439CODEN: DTARAF; ISSN:1477-9226.
(Royal Society of Chemistry)

A set of alkynyl-tris(2-pyridyl)phosphine Au(I) complexes was synthesized and
characterized. Free coordination functions on the ligand environment
periphery, namely 'scorpionate' PPy3 and the C≡C bond, allowed these ditopic
metalloligands to be selectively linked to 1D coordination polymers by
reaction with Cu(I), which used both Cu-(N-PPy3) and Cu-(η2-C≡C) coordination
modes. Single-crystal and powder XRD, NMR, and XPS techniques were used to
characterize the coordination polymers obtained. Heterobimetallic Au(I)-Cu(I)
coordination polymers demonstrate triplet photoluminescence which was studied
by spectroscopic and computational methods to understand the pathway of energy
transfer inside the chain of linked chromophore centers. The intriguing
feature of the electronic structure of heterobimetallic supramol. assemblies
is the 'long-distance' electronic transition involving PhC2 and PPy3 ligands
located at a distance of more than 1 nm from each other. Thus, the assembly of
a heterobimetallic coordination polymer from relatively simple 'building
blocks' retains the block-wise nature of the electronic structure, but the
photophys. properties of the polymer are fundamentally different from the
properties of discrete organometallic components.

Nitronyl nitroxide as a coupling partner: Pd-mediated cross-coupling of
(nitronyl nitroxide-2-ido)(triphenylphosphine)gold(I) with aryl halides

Tanimoto, Ryu; Suzuki, Shuichi; Kozaki, Masatoshi; Okada, Keiji

Chemistry Letters (2014), 43 (5), 678-680CODEN: CMLTAG; ISSN:0366-7022.
(Chemical Society of Japan)

A cross-coupling reaction using nitronyl nitroxide (NN) as a coupling partner
was developed. Refluxing a mixt. of [AuI(NN-2-ido)(PPh3)] and aryl iodide in
THF in the presence of 10 mol % [Pd(PPh3)4] produced a cross-coupling
products, e.g., I, in a high yield. This method allowed the direct
introduction of the NN radical onto various poly- and heterocyclic arom.
rings.

13

An efficient synthetic method for organometallic radicals: structures and
properties of gold(I)-(nitronyl nitroxide)-2-ide complexes

Suzuki, Shuichi; Kira, Sayaka; Kozaki, Masatoshi; Yamamura, Masaki; Hasegawa,
Toru; Nabeshima, Tatsuya; Okada, Keiji

Dalton Transactions (2017), 46 (8), 2653-2659CODEN: DTARAF; ISSN:1477-9226.
(Royal Society of Chemistry)

One-pot synthesis of (nitronyl nitroxide)-gold(I)-phosphine (NN-Au-P)
complexes has been developed using chloro(tetrahydrothiophene)gold(I),
phosphine ligands, nitronyl nitroxide radicals, and sodium hydroxide. The NN-
Au-P complexes can be easily handled because they were quite stable under
aerated conditions in both soln. and cryst. states. They showed weak
absorption bands with vibrational structures in the 450-650 nm region. The
oxidn. potentials assigned to the NN moieties of NN-Au-P complexes with arom.
phosphines were obsd. around -0.1 V vs. Fc/Fc+ (-0.11 V for NN-Au-1, -0.08 V
for NN-Au-2, -0.13 V for NN-Au-5, and -0.07 V for NN-Au-6), somewhat lower
than that of NN-Au-P complexes with aliph. phosphines (-0.25 V for NN-Au-3 and
-0.17 V for NN-Au-4).

14

Synthesis and structure of (nitronyl nitroxide-2-ido)(tert-
butyldiphenylphosphine)gold(I) and -(Di(tert-butyl)phenylphosphine)gold(I)
derivatives; their comparative study in the cross-coupling reaction

Zayakin, Igor; Bagryanskaya, Irina; Stass, Dmitri; Kazantsev, Maxim;
Tretyakov, Evgeny

Crystals (2020), 10 (9), 770CODEN: CRYSBC; ISSN:2073-4352. (MDPI AG)

We synthesized two new organogold derivs. of a nitronyl nitroxide (NN) with
phosphine ligands, namely NN-Au-PtBuPh2 and NN-Au-PtBu2Ph. They were
characterized by X-ray diffraction anal., cyclic voltammetry, and ESR, IR, and
UV/Vis spectroscopy. The X-ray structural anal. revealed a tendency of the NN
moiety to form a large no. of short intermol. contacts. This phenomenon is
related to the anionic nature of the paramagnetic group NN, as evidenced by a
significantly lower oxidn. potential in comparison with purely org. derivs. of
NN radicals. The cross-coupling reaction of NN-Au-PPh3, NN-Au-PtBuPh2, or NN-
Au-PtBu2Ph with an activated bromoarene, namely, p-BrC6H4NO2, was
investigated. It was shown that regardless of the presence of the bulky tert-
Bu substituent, all gold derivs. have similar activities in the cross-coupling
reaction and give a cross-coupling product, NN-C6H4NO2, with comparable
yields.

15

Synthesis, structure and properties of nitronyl nitroxide diradicals with
fused-thiophene couplers

Tretyakov, Evgeny; Okada, Keiji; Suzuki, Shuichi; Baumgarten, Martin;
Romanenko, Galina; Bogomyakov, Artem; Ovcharenko, Victor

Journal of Physical Organic Chemistry (2016), 29 (12), 725-734CODEN: JPOCEE;
ISSN:0894-3230. (John Wiley & Sons Ltd.)

Functionalized diradicals are widely used in chem., physics, biol. and
material sciences because of their inherently high reactivity and magnetic
activity, but the synthesis of planar diradical mols. is still a challenge.
This paper describes the effective synthesis and properties of diradicals in
which nitronyl nitroxide fragments and a fused-thiophene bridge are almost
coplanar. The diradicals
2,2'-thieno[2,3-b]thiophene-2,5-diylbis[4,5-dihydro-4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1H-Imidazol-1-yloxy
3,3'-dioxide,
2,2'-thieno[3,2-b]thiophene-2,5-diylbis[4,5-dihydro-4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1H-Imidazol-1-yloxy
3,3'-dioxide,
2,2'-thieno[2,3-b]thiophene-2,5-diylbis[4,5-dihydro-4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1H-Imidazol-1-yloxy
3,3'-dioxide, and
2,2'-dithieno[3,2-b:2',3'-d]thiophene-2,6-diylbis[4,5-dihydro-4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1H-Imidazol-1-yloxy
3,3'-dioxide (I, II, and III) were prepd. in high yield by palladium-catalyzed
cross-coupling of the (nitronyl nitroxide-2-ide)(triphenylphosphin)gold
complex with 2,5-diiodothieno[2,3-b]thiophene,
2,5-diiodothieno[3,2-b]thiophene or 2,6-diiododithieno[3,2-b:2',3'-d]thiophene
correspondingly. The torsion angles between planes of the nitronyl nitroxide
fragments and fused-thiophene systems lie in the range 2.6-8.5°. The planarity
of and makes the Kekule-type coupler units effective bridges to transmit
magnetic interactions by a spin polarization mechanism. According to the
magnetic susceptibility measurements, supported by d. functional theory
calcns., the singlet-triplet energy splitting (2 J/kB) is -130 K for II and
close to -80 K for III. The singlet diradicals and are stable and demonstrate
two 1-electron reversible electrochem. oxidn. peaks and two-electron quasi-
reversible redn. peaks giving the corresponding bis(oxoammonium) dication or
bis(aminooxidanide) dianion. Copyright © 2016 John Wiley and Sons, Ltd.

16

The Design of Radical Stacks: Nitronyl-Nitroxide-Substituted Heteropentacenes

Tretyakov, Evgeny; Keerthi, Ashok; Baumgarten, Martin; Veber, Sergey; Fedin,
Matvey; Gorbunov, Dmitry; Shundrina, Inna; Gritsan, Nina

ChemistryOpen (2017), 6 (5), 642-652CODEN: CHOPCK; ISSN:2191-1363. (Wiley-VCH
Verlag GmbH & Co. KGaA)

The first alkyl chain-anchored heteropentacene,
dithieno[2,3-d;2',3'-d']benzo-[1,2-b;3,4-b']dithiophene (DTmBDT), mono- or
disubstituted with a nitronyl nitroxide group has been prepd. through a cross-
coupling synthetic procedure of the corresponding dibromo-deriv. (Br2-DTmBDT)
with a nitronyl nitroxide-2-ide gold(I) complex. The synthesized nitroxides
possess high kinetic stability, which allowed us to investigate their
structure and thermal, optical, electrochem., and magnetic properties. Single-
crystal X-ray diffraction of both mono- and diradicals revealed that the
nitronyl nitroxide group lies almost in the same plane as the nearest side
thiophene ring. Such arrangement favors formation of edge-to-edge dimers,
which then form close π-stacks surrounded by interdigitating alkyl chains.
Before melting, these nitronyl nitroxide radical substituted mols. undergo at
least two different phase transitions (PTs): for the monoradical, PTs are
reversible, accompanied by hysteresis, and occur near 13 and 83°C; the
diradical upon heating shows a reversible PT with hysteresis in the temp.
range 2-11°C and an irreversible PT near 135°C. PTs of this type are absent in
Br2-DTmBDT. Therefore, the step-by-step substitution of bromine atoms by
nitronyl nitroxide groups changes the structural organization of DTmBDT and
induces the emergence of PTs. This knowledge may facilitate crystal
engineering of π-stacked paramagnets and related mol.spin devices.

(Azulene-1,3-diyl)-bis(nitronyl nitroxide) and
(Azulene-1,3-diyl)-bis(iminonitroxide) and Their Copper Complexes

Haraguchi, Makoto; Tretyakov, Evgeny; Gritsan, Nina; Romanenko, Galina;
Gorbunov, Dmitry; Bogomyakov, Artem; Maryunina, Kseniya; Suzuki, Shuichi;
Kozaki, Masatoshi; Shiomi, Daisuke; Sato, Kazunobu; Takui, Takeji; Nishihara,
Sadafumi; Inoue, Katsuya; Okada, Keiji

Chemistry - An Asian Journal (2017), 12 (22), 2929-2941CODEN: CAAJBI;
ISSN:1861-4728. (Wiley-VCH Verlag GmbH & Co. KGaA)

In contrast to diradicals connected by alternant hydrocarbons, only a few
studies on those connected by nonalternant hydrocarbons have been reported.
The syntheses, structures, and magnetic properties of azulene-1,3-diyl linked
bis(nitronyl nitroxide) (NN2Az) and bis(iminonitroxide) (IN2Az) diradicals and
their Cu(hfac)2 (hfac = hexafluoroacetylacetonate) complexes were
investigated. NN2Az has an intramol. ferromagnetic interaction with
Jobs/kB=+10.0 K (H = -2JS1·S2) between (nitronyl nitroxide) spins, whereas
IN2Az has a much weaker intramol. magnetic interaction. The reactions of NN2Az
and IN2Az with Cu(hfac)2 gave a 1:2 [{Cu(hfac)2}2(NN2Az)] complex and a 1:1
[Cu(hfac)2(IN2Az)]·C6H12 complex, resp. [{Cu(hfac)2}2(NN2Az)] showed strong
intramol. antiferromagnetic interactions (J1-Cu-R/kB≈-800 K, J2-Cu-R/kB≈-500
K) between the CuII spins and the coordinating NN spins, whereas
[Cu(hfac)2(IN2Az)] exhibited a ferromagnetic exchange interaction (Jobs-
Cu-R/kB=+114 K) between the CuII spin and the imino-coordinated iminonitroxide
spin.

1,3-Diaza[3]ferrocenophanes functionalized with a nitronyl nitroxide group

Gurskaya, Larisa; Bagryanskaya, Irina; Amosov, Evgeny; Kazantsev, Maxim;
Politanskaya, Larisa; Zaytseva, Elena; Bagryanskaya, Elena; Chernonosov,
Aleksander; Tretyakov, Evgeny

Tetrahedron (2018), 74 (15), 1942-1950CODEN: TETRAB; ISSN:0040-4020. (Elsevier
Ltd.)

An aza-Wittig reaction of 1,1'-bis(triphenylphosphoranylidenamino)ferrocene
with 4-iodo-, 2,5-difluoro-4-iodo-, pentafluoro-, or 4-methoxycarbonylphenyl
isocyanates is described here. Intermediate betaines, which are products of a
nucleophilic attack of the N atom of the remaining iminophosphorane moiety on
the C atom of the carbodiimide functional group, were isolated; their
structures were proved by monocrystal x-ray diffraction. Hydrolysis of the
betaines under mild conditions gave corresponding
arylamino-1,3-diaza[3]ferrocenophanes. However, as demonstrated, under more
rigid conditions, the betaines could be converted into ferrocenophanes with
the 1,3-diazetidine-2,4-diimine coupler. The iodo- or diiodo-derivs. of
1,3-diaza[3]ferrocenophanes were cross-coupled with a nitronyl nitroxide-2-ide
Au(I) complex to obtain corresponding metallocenophanes functionalized with
the nitronyl nitroxide group(s). The mol. and crystal structures of the
halogenated and spin-labeled ferrocenophanes were solved by x-ray anal. In
arylamino-1,3-diaza[3]ferrocenophanes, according to cyclic voltammetry, the
position of the quasi-reversible oxidn. peak assocd. with the Fe(II)/Fe(III)
redox couple deviates within 0.09 V depending on substituents in the arylamino
group.

Molecular and Crystal Structure of 2-Amino-
Polyfluorophenyl-4,4,5,5-Tetramethyl-4,5-Dihydro-1H-Imidazol- 3-Oxide-1-Oxyls

Tretyakov, E. V.; Makhneva, T. V.; Politanskaya, L. V.; Bagryanskaya, I. Yu.;
Stass, D. V.

Journal of Structural Chemistry (2018), 59 (3), 689-696CODEN: JSTCAM;
ISSN:0022-4766. (Springer)

By cross-coupling of 2-iodo-polyfluoroanilines with a [AuPPh3NN] complex
(where NN is 4,4,5,5- tetramethyl-3-oxide-1-oxyl-2-imidazolin-2-ide) in the
presence of [Pd(PPh3)4], nitroxide radicals (2-aminopolyfluorophenyl-
4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-4,5-dihydro-1H-imidazol-3-oxide-1-oxyls) are synthesized.
Their mol. and crystal structures are detd. by the X-ray crystallog. anal. It
is shown that in the solid phase there are both intra- and intermol. hydrogen
bonds between NH2 groups and O atoms of paramagnetic fragments, which link the
mols. into chains or dimers.

20

Magnetic edge states and coherent manipulation of graphene nanoribbons

Slota, Michael; Keerthi, Ashok; Myers, William K.; Tretyakov, Evgeny;
Baumgarten, Martin; Ardavan, Arzhang; Sadeghi, Hatef; Lambert, Colin J.;
Narita, Akimitsu; Mullen, Klaus; Bogani, Lapo

Nature (London, United Kingdom) (2018), 557 (7707), 691-695CODEN: NATUAS;
ISSN:0028-0836. (Nature Research)

Graphene, a single-layer network of carbon atoms, has outstanding elec. and
mech. properties1. Graphene ribbons with nanometer-scale widths2,3
(nanoribbons) should exhibit half-metallicity4 and quantum confinement.
Magnetic edges in graphene nanoribbons5,6 have been studied extensively from a
theor. standpoint because their coherent manipulation would be a milestone for
spintronic7 and quantum computing devices8. However, exptl. investigations
have been hampered because nanoribbon edges cannot be produced with at.
precision and the graphene terminations that have been proposed are chem.
unstable9. Here we address both of these problems, by using mol. graphene
nanoribbons functionalized with stable spin-bearing radical groups. We observe
the predicted delocalized magnetic edge states and test theor. models of the
spin dynamics and spin-environment interactions. Comparison with a non-
graphitized ref. material enables us to clearly identify the characteristic
behavior of the radical-functionalized graphene nanoribbons. We quantify the
parameters of spin-orbit coupling, define the interaction patterns and det.
the spin decoherence channels. Even without any optimization, the spin
coherence time is in the range of microseconds at room temp., and we perform
quantum inversion operations between edge and radical spins. Our approach
provides a way of testing the theory of magnetism in graphene nanoribbons
exptl. The coherence times that we observe open up encouraging prospects for
the use of magnetic nanoribbons in quantum spintronic devices.

21

Ferromagnetically Coupled S=1 Chains in Crystals of Verdazyl-Nitronyl
Nitroxide Diradicals

Tretyakov Evgeny V; Zhivetyeva Svetlana I; Bagryanskaya Irina Yu; Kazantsev
Maxim S; Shundrina Inna K; Zaytseva Elena V; Parkhomenko Dmitriy A;
Bagryanskaya Elena G; Tretyakov Evgeny V; Gorbunov Dmitry E; Gritsan Nina P;
Bagryanskaya Irina Yu; Kazantsev Maxim S; Zaytseva Elena V; Parkhomenko
Dmitriy A; Bagryanskaya Elena G; Petunin Pavel V; Postnikov Pavel S; Trusova
Marina E; Gorbunov Dmitry E; Gritsan Nina P; Bogomyakov Artem S; Ovcharenko
Victor I; Postnikov Pavel S

Angewandte Chemie (International ed. in English) (2020), 59 (46), 20704-20710
ISSN:.

Thermally stable organic diradicals with a triplet ground state along with
large singlet-triplet energy gap have significant potential for advanced
technological applications. A series of phenylene-bridged diradicals with
oxoverdazyl and nitronyl nitroxide units were synthesized via a palladium-
catalyzed cross-coupling reaction of iodoverdazyls with a nitronyl
nitroxide-2-ide gold(I) complex with high yields. The diradicals exhibit high
stability and do not decompose in an inert atmosphere up to 180 °C. For the
diradicals, both substantial AF (ΔEST ≈-64 cm(-1) ) and FM (ΔEST ≥25 and 100
cm(-1) ) intramolecular exchange interactions were observed. The sign of the
exchange interaction is determined both by the bridging moiety (para- or meta-
phenylene) and by the type of oxoverdazyl block (C-linked or N-linked). Upon
crystallization, diradicals with the triplet ground state form unique one-
dimensional exchange-coupled chains with strong intra- and weak inter-
diradical ferromagnetic coupling.

22

2-(8-Iodonaphthalen-1-yl)-Substituted Nitronyl Nitroxide: Suppressed
Reactivity of Iodine Atom and Unusual Temperature Dynamics of the EPR Spectrum

Tretyakov, Evgeny V.; Lomanovich, Konstantin A.; Bagryanskaya, Elena G.;
Romanenko, Galina V.; Bogomyakov, Artem S.; Zueva, Ekaterina M.; Petrova,
Maria M.; Dmitriev, Alexey A.; Gritsan, Nina P.

European Journal of Organic Chemistry (2021), 2021 (17), 2355-2361CODEN:
EJOCFK; ISSN:1099-0690. (Wiley-VCH Verlag GmbH & Co. KGaA)

While developing methods for obtaining high-spin graphene nanostructures, the
authors carried out a model cross-coupling reaction between
1,8-diiodonaphthalene and an organogold deriv. of a nitronyl nitroxide. It was
found that only one iodine atom reacted, leading to the corresponding
8-iodonaphthalen-1-yl-substituted nitronyl nitroxide. The latter was
successfully isolated as two polymorphic modifications, whose X-ray structural
anal. revealed a strong distortion of the paramagnet's geometry in comparison
with a noniodinated analog. Moreover, the spatial proximity of the heavy
iodine atom and the paramagnetic moiety resulted in unprecedented temp.
dynamics of the EPR spectrum. To clarify these dynamics, quantum chem. calcns.
were performed using the exact two-component relativistic X2C method with the
self-consistent account of spin-orbit coupling in conjunction with the B3LYP
functional and relativistic STO-type basis set. These computations predicted a
reasonable aiso(127I) value that can affect the EPR spectrum. Substitution of
the second iodine atom would lead to the formation of a diradical with a
1,8-naphthalenediyl bridge, whose structure and magnetic properties were
analyzed at the DFT level. Two stable conformations of the diradical with
strongly distorted geometry were identified. In both conformations, calcns.
predict a ferromagnetic exchange between the paramagnetic centers with J=8 and
14 cm-1 (H=-2 J·S1S2).

23

Platform for High-Spin Molecules: A Verdazyl-Nitronyl Nitroxide Triradical
with Quartet Ground State

Tretyakov, Evgeny V.; Petunin, Pavel V.; Zhivetyeva, Svetlana I.; Gorbunov,
Dmitry E.; Gritsan, Nina P.; Fedin, Matvey V.; Stass, Dmitri V.; Samoilova,
Rimma I.; Bagryanskaya, Irina Yu.; Shundrina, Inna K.; Bogomyakov, Artem S.;
Kazantsev, Maxim S.; Postnikov, Pavel S.; Trusova, Marina E.; Ovcharenko,
Victor I.

Journal of the American Chemical Society (2021), 143 (21), 8164-8176CODEN:
JACSAT; ISSN:0002-7863. (American Chemical Society)

Thermally resistant air-stable org. triradicals with a quartet ground state
and a large energy gap between spin states are still unique compds. In this
work, we succeeded to design and prep. the first highly stable triradical,
consisting of oxoverdazyl and nitronyl nitroxide radical fragments, with a
quartet ground state. The triradical I and its diradical precursor II were
synthesized via a palladium-catalyzed cross-coupling reaction of
diiodoverdazyl with nitronyl nitroxide-2-ide gold(I) complex. Both
paramagnetic compds. were fully characterized by single-crystal X-ray
diffraction anal., superconducting quantum interference device magnetometry,
EPR spectroscopy in various matrixes, and cyclic voltammetry. In the
diradical, the verdazyl and nitronyl nitroxide centers demonstrated full
reversibility of redox process, while for the triradical, the electrochem.
redn. and oxidn. proceed at practically the same redox potentials, but become
quasi-reversible. A series of high-level CASSCF/NEVPT2 calcns. was performed
to predict inter- and intramol. exchange interactions in crystals of di- and
triradicals and to establish their magnetic motifs. Based on the predicted
magnetic motifs, the temp. dependences of the magnetic susceptibility were
analyzed, and the singlet-triplet (135 ± 10 cm-1) and doublet-quartet (17 ± 2
and 152 ± 19 cm-1) splitting was found to be moderate. Unique high stability
of synthesized verdazyl-nitronylnitroxide triradical opens new perspectives
for further functionalization and design of high-spin systems with four or
more spins.

26

Aurophilic Interactions in Multi-Radical Species: Electronic-Spin and Redox
Properties of Bis- and Tris-[(Nitronyl Nitroxide)-Gold(I)] Complexes with
Phosphine-Ligand Scaffolds

Kira, Sayaka; Miyamae, Takayuki; Yoshida, Kohei; Kanzaki, Yuki; Sugisaki,
Kenji; Shiomi, Daisuke; Sato, Kazunobu; Takui, Takeji; Suzuki, Shuichi;
Kozaki, Masatoshi; Okada, Keiji

Chemistry - A European Journal (2021), 27 (44), 11450-11457CODEN: CEUJED;
ISSN:0947-6539. (Wiley-VCH Verlag GmbH & Co. KGaA)

Multinuclear AuI complexes with two or three nitronyl 2-nitroxide (NN, NN =
4,4,5,5-tetramethyl-1H-imidazol-2-yl N,N-dioxide) radical anion and phosphine-
ligand scaffolds, (NN-Au)2-1,2-(PPh2)2C6H4 (1o), (NN-Au)2-1,3-(PPh2)2C6H4
(1m), and (NN-Au)2-1,4-(PPh2)2C6H4 (1p), have been synthesized to investigate
the influence of AuI-AuI (aurophilic) interactions on the properties of
multispin mol. systems. The desired complexes were successfully prepd. in
moderate yields in a one-pot synthesis from the corresponding phosphine
ligand, AuI source, parent NN, and sodium hydroxide. Among the prepd.
complexes, (NN-Au)2-1o, in which an aurophilic interaction was clearly obsd.
by crystal structure anal., showed characteristic spin-spin interactions,
electrochem. properties, and solvatochromic behavior. The results from theor.
calcns. also suggested that the differences in properties between complex (NN-
Au)2-1o and the other complexes are due to intramol. aurophilic interactions.

28

Simulation of multiple isotropic spin-trap EPR spectra

Duling, David R.

Journal of Magnetic Resonance, Series B (1994), 104 (2), 105-10CODEN: JMRBE5;
ISSN:1064-1866.

A computer program has been developed for fitting EPR data with multiple free
radicals as formed in biochem. and chem. spin-trapping systems. Simulation of
these spectra requires as many as 40 independent parameters, creating a
chaotic anal. environment. Accurate simulation of these systems is essential
for cor. identification of the free radicals which often show only slightly
differences in spin-Hamiltonian parameters. This method consists of rule-based
perturbations with trial and error calcns. and has proven successful in
several applications. Details of the algorithm, example data, and a discussion
of the difficulties of this anal. are presented in this report.

Optically Detected ESR and Low Magnetic Field Signals from Spin Triads:
2-Imidazoline-1-Oxyl Derivatives in X-irradiated Alkane Liquids as a Method to
Study Three-Spin Systems

Sviridenko, Fyodor B.; Stass, Dmitri V.; Kobzeva, Tatyana V.; Tretyakov,
Evgeny V.; Klyatskaya, Svetlana V.; Mshvidobadze, Elena V.; Vasilevsky, Sergey
F.; Molin, Yuri N.

Journal of the American Chemical Society (2004), 126 (9), 2807-2819CODEN:
JACSAT; ISSN:0002-7863. (American Chemical Society)

This contribution reports the design and synthesis of spin-labeled charge
acceptors to produce three-spin systems of radical ion/biradical ion type in
X-irradiated alkane liqs. This opens the way to study spin triads in exptl.
conditions, in which short-lived radical ion pairs are conventionally studied,
thus offering optically detected techniques such as magneto-resonance OD ESR
and level-crossing MARY spectroscopy. The structure of the synthesized
2-imidazoline-1-oxyl derivs. is A-Sp-R, where A is a pos. or neg. charge
acceptor, R is a stable radical, and Sp is a hydrocarbon bridge. The set of
20+ compds. represent a convenient tool to construct exptl. three-spin systems
with various properties, e.g.,with the third spin introduced into one or the
other partner of the radical ion pair. The degree of exchange coupling between
the two paramagnetic fragments in the biradical ion strongly depends on the
type of the radical fragment R and the structure of the bridge Sp. As a
result, acceptors with systematically reduced exchange interaction were
synthesized, and optimal systems for the observation of low magnetic field
effect were found. In the most favorable case, an OD ESR signal from a spin
triad living as short as ca. 100 Ns was registered as a single unresolved
line. The exchange integral for this biradical anion (9) was estd. from OD ESR
and ESR expts. to be ∼103 G by the order of magnitude, which is much greater
than the hyperfine couplings in the biradical ion but much smaller than the
thermal energy kT.

OLEX2: a complete structure solution, refinement and analysis program

Dolomanov, Oleg V.; Bourhis, Luc J.; Gildea, Richard J.; Howard, Judith A. K.;

mol. crystal structures. The software has a portable mouse-driven workflow-

both chemists and crystallographers.

33

Semiempirical GGA-type density functional constructed with a long-range
dispersion correction

Grimme, Stefan

Journal of Computational Chemistry (2006), 27 (15), 1787-1799CODEN: JCCHDD;
ISSN:0192-8651. (John Wiley & Sons, Inc.)

A new d. functional (DF) of the generalized gradient approxn. (GGA) type for
general chem. applications termed B97-D is proposed. It is based on Becke's
power-series ansatz from 1997 and is explicitly parameterized by including
damped atom-pairwise dispersion corrections of the form C6·R-6. A general
computational scheme for the parameters used in this correction has been
established and parameters for elements up to xenon and a scaling factor for
the dispersion part for several common d. functionals (BLYP, PBE, TPSS, B3LYP)
are reported. The new functional is tested in comparison with other GGAs and
the B3LYP hybrid functional on std. thermochem. benchmark sets, for 40
noncovalently bound complexes, including large stacked arom. mols. and group
II element clusters, and for the computation of mol. geometries. Further
cross-validation tests were performed for organometallic reactions and other
difficult problems for std. functionals. In summary, it is found that B97-D
belongs to one of the most accurate general purpose GGAs, reaching, for
example for the G97/2 set of heat of formations, a mean abs. deviation of only
3.8 kcal mol-1. The performance for noncovalently bound systems including many
pure van der Waals complexes is exceptionally good, reaching on the av.
CCSD(T) accuracy. The basic strategy in the development to restrict the d.
functional description to shorter electron correlation lengths scales and to
describe situations with medium to large interat. distances by damped C6·R-6
terms seems to be very successful, as demonstrated for some notoriously
difficult reactions. As an example, for the isomerization of larger branched
to linear alkanes, B97-D is the only DF available that yields the right sign
for the energy difference. From a practical point of view, the new functional
seems to be quite robust and it is thus suggested as an efficient and accurate
quantum chem. method for large systems where dispersion forces are of general
importance.
